  &#x20;[Bandits](658200.md) began attacking caravans by 199, leading Palmyra to strengthen its military presence.

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA28_28\]-263]

- The new dynasty favored the city,

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA28_28\]-263]

  &#x20;stationing the [Cohors I Flavia Chalcidenorum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auxilia#Cohortes) garrison there by 206.

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEdwell2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDQgmOZlsEWcCpgPA60_60\]-264]

  &#x20;[Caracalla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caracalla) made Palmyra a [colonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonia_\(Roman\)) between 213 and 216, replacing many Greek institutions with Roman constitutional ones.

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTESartre2005\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidMNSyT_PuYVMCpgPA512_512\]-262]

  &#x20;[Severus Alexander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Severus_Alexander), emperor from 222 to 235, visited Palmyra in 229.

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA28_28\]-263]

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTETeixidor1979\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidTccUAAAAIAAJpgPR33_33\]-265]

#### Palmyrene kingdom

See also: [List of Palmyrene monarchs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Palmyrene_monarchs)

The rise of the [Sasanian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sasanian_Empire) in Persia considerably damaged Palmyrene trade.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA176_176\]-266] The Sasanians disbanded Palmyrene colonies in their lands,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA176_176\]-266] and began a war against the Roman Empire.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA29_29\]-267] In an inscription dated to 252 [Odaenathus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odaenathus) appears bearing the title of [exarchos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exarch) (lord) of Palmyra.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidecfiAAAAQBAJpgPA44_44\]-268] The weakness of the Roman Empire and the constant Persian danger were probably the reasons behind the Palmyrene council's decision to elect a lord for the city in order for him to lead a strengthened army.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBall2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid73-JAgAAQBAJpgPA77_77\]-269] Odaenathus approached [Shapur I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shapur_I) of Persia to request him to guarantee Palmyrene interests in Persia, but was rebuffed.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA177_177\]-270] In 260 the Emperor [Valerian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valerian_\(emperor\)) fought Shapur at the [Battle of Edessa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Edessa), but was defeated and captured.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA177_177\]-270] One of Valerian's officers, [Macrianus Major](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macrianus_Major), his sons [Quietus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quietus) and [Macrianus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macrianus_Minor), and the [prefect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Praetorian_prefect) [Balista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balista) rebelled against Valerian's son [Gallienus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallienus), usurping imperial power in Syria.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDrinkwater2005\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidMNSyT_PuYVMCpgPA44_44\]-271]

##### Persian wars

clay piece bearing a depiction of a king wearing a diadem and earring

A clay tessera bearing a possible depiction of Odaenathus wearing a diadem

Odaenathus formed an army of Palmyrenes and Syrian peasants against Shapur.[^cite_note-273][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA177_177\]-270] According to the *[Augustan History](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Augustan_History)*, Odaenathus declared himself king prior to the battle.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDignasWinter2007\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidMG2hqcRDvJgCpgPA159_159\]-274] The Palmyrene leader won a decisive victory near the banks of the Euphrates later in 260 forcing the Persians to retreat.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHartmann2001139-275] In 261 Odaenathus marched against the remaining usurpers in Syria, defeating and killing Quietus and Balista.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHartmann2001144,_145-276] As a reward, he received the title *Imperator Totius Orientis* ("Governor of the East") from Gallienus,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidwnTOBAAAQBAJpgPA67_67\]-277] and ruled Syria, [Mesopotamia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesopotamia_\(Roman_province\)), [Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabia_Petraea) and [Anatolia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anatolia)'s eastern regions as the imperial representative.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDe_Blois1976\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid7-jUAMmMS5cCpgPA35_35\]-278][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAndrade2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidy6IaBQAAQBAJpgPA333_333\]-279] Palmyra itself remained officially part of the empire but Palmyrene inscriptions started to describe it as a "metrocolonia", indicating that the city's status was higher than normal Roman colonias.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYoung2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidE5yCAgAAQBAJpgPA215_215\]-280] In practice, Palmyra shifted from a provincial city to a de facto allied kingdom.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYoung2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidE5yCAgAAQBAJpgPA159_159\]-281]

In 262 Odaenathus launched a new campaign against Shapur,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAndo2012\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid2fTcCQAAQBAJpgPA237_237\]-282] reclaiming the rest of Roman Mesopotamia (most importantly, the cities of [Nisibis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nisibis) and [Carrhae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carrhae)), sacking the Jewish city of [Nehardea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nehardea),[^cite_note-284][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDubnov1968\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidLLCXomFNU3cCpgPA151_151\]-283][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHartmann2001\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidBdcHK8Ll1jMCpgPA171_171\]-285] and [besieging](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Ctesiphon_\(263\)) the Persian capital [Ctesiphon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ctesiphon).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDignasWinter2007\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidMG2hqcRDvJgCpgPA160_160\]-286][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHartmann2001\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidBdcHK8Ll1jMCpgPA172_172\]-287] Following his victory, the Palmyrene monarch assumed the title [King of Kings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_of_Kings).[^cite_note-290][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatson2004\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidkJ2JAgAAQBAJpgPA32_32\]-291] Later, Odaenathus crowned his son [Hairan I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hairan_I) as co-King of Kings near Antioch in 263.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHartmann2001\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidBdcHK8Ll1jMCpgPA176_176\]-292] Although he did not take the Persian capital, Odaenathus drove the Persians out of all Roman lands conquered since the beginning of Shapur's [wars in 252](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Nisibis_\(252\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDe_Blois1976\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid7-jUAMmMS5cCpgPA3_3\]-293] In a second campaign that took place in 266, the Palmyrene king reached Ctesiphon again; however, he had to leave the siege and move north, accompanied by Hairan I, to repel [Gothic attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia_\(Roman_province\)#Decline) on [Asia Minor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia_Minor).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidwnTOBAAAQBAJpgPA76_76\]-294] The king and his son were assassinated during their return in 267;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidwnTOBAAAQBAJpgPA77_77\]-295] according to the *Augustan History* and [Joannes Zonaras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joannes_Zonaras), Odaenathus was killed by a cousin (Zonaras says nephew) named in the *History* as [Maeonius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maeonius).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDqMrR29Cc7MCpgPA78_78\]-296] The *Augustan History* also says that Maeonius was proclaimed emperor for a brief period before being killed by the soldiers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDqMrR29Cc7MCpgPA78_78\]-296][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA292_292\]-297][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStoneman1994\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid8kLFfE1qPhICpgPA108_108\]-298] However, no inscriptions or other evidence exist for Maeonius' reign.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrauer1975\[httpsarchiveorgdetailsageofsoldierem00brau_163\]-299]



Zenobia as Augusta, on the obverse of an Antoninianus.

Odaenathus was succeeded by his son; the ten-year-old [Vaballathus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaballathus).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA299_299\]-300] [Zenobia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zenobia), the mother of the new king, was the *de facto* ruler and Vaballathus remained in her shadow while she consolidated her power.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA299_299\]-300] Gallienus dispatched his prefect [Heraclian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aurelius_Heraclianus) to command military operations against the Persians, but he was marginalized by Zenobia and returned to the West.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDe_Blois1976\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid7-jUAMmMS5cCpgPA3_3\]-293] The queen was careful not to provoke Rome, claiming for herself and her son the titles held by her husband while guaranteeing the safety of the borders with Persia and pacifying the [Tanukhids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanukhids) in [Hauran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hauran).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA299_299\]-300] To protect the borders with Persia, Zenobia fortified different settlements on the Euphrates including the citadels of [Halabiye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halabiye) and [Zalabiye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zalabiye).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidwnTOBAAAQBAJpgPA91_91\]-301] Circumstantial evidence exist for confrontations with the Sasanians; probably in 269 Vaballathus took the title *Persicus Maximus* ("The great victor in Persia") and the title might be linked with an unrecorded battle against a Persian army trying to regain control of Northern Mesopotamia.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidwnTOBAAAQBAJpgPA92_92\]-302][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHartmann2001\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidBdcHK8Ll1jMCpgPA267_267\]-303]

##### Palmyrene empire

Main article: [Palmyrene Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palmyrene_Empire)

Map of the Palmyrene empire

The Palmyrene empire in AD 271

Zenobia began her military career in the spring of 270, during the reign of [Claudius Gothicus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claudius_Gothicus).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA302_302\]-304] Under the pretext of attacking the Tanukhids, she conquered Roman Arabia.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA302_302\]-304] This was followed in October by an [invasion of Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palmyrene_invasion_of_Egypt),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatson2004\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidkJ2JAgAAQBAJpgPA62_62\]-305][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA303_303\]-306] ending with a Palmyrene victory and Zenobia's proclamation as queen of Egypt.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA304_304\]-307] Palmyra invaded Anatolia the following year, reaching [Ankara](802.md) and the pinnacle of its expansion.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBall2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid73-JAgAAQBAJpgPA80_80\]-308] The conquests were made behind a mask of subordination to Rome.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA179_179\]-309] Zenobia issued coins in the name of Claudius' successor [Aurelian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aurelian), with Vaballathus depicted as king;[^cite_note-310][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA179_179\]-309] since Aurelian was occupied with repelling insurgencies in Europe, he tolerated the Palmyrene coinage and encroachments.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatson2004\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidkJ2JAgAAQBAJpgPA67_67\]-311][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDqMrR29Cc7MCpgPA118_118\]-312] In late 271, Vaballathus and his mother assumed the titles of [Augustus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Augustus_\(honorific\)) (emperor) and [Augusta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Augustae).[^cite_note-316][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA179_179\]-309]

The following year, Aurelian crossed the [Bosphorus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosphorus) and advanced quickly through Anatolia.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA307_307\]-317] According to one account, Roman general [Marcus Aurelius Probus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcus_Aurelius_Probus) regained Egypt from Palmyra;[^cite_note-319][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA308_308\]-318] Aurelian entered [Issus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Issus_\(town\)) and headed to [Antioch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antioch), where he defeated Zenobia in the [Battle of Immae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Immae).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA309_309\]-320] Zenobia was defeated again at the [Battle of Emesa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Emesa), taking refuge in [Homs](77533.md) before quickly returning to her capital.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA310_310\]-321] When the Romans besieged Palmyra, Zenobia refused their order to surrender in person to the emperor.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBall2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid73-JAgAAQBAJpgPA80_80\]-308] She escaped east to ask the Persians for help, but was captured by the Romans; the city capitulated soon afterwards.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBall2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid73-JAgAAQBAJpgPA81_81\]-322][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDrinkwater2005\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidMNSyT_PuYVMCpgPA52_52\]-323]

#### Later Roman and Byzantine periods

Ruins, with columns and arches

Diocletian's camp

Aurelian spared the city and stationed a garrison of 600 archers, led by [Sandarion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandarion?action=edit\&redlink=1), as a peacekeeping force.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA313_313\]-324] In 273 Palmyra rebelled under the leadership of [Septimius Apsaios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Septimius_Apsaios?action=edit\&redlink=1),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA180_180\]-315] declaring [Antiochus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Septimius_Antiochus) (a relative of Zenobia) as Augustus.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA181_181\]-325] Aurelian marched against Palmyra, razing it to the ground and seizing the most valuable monuments to decorate his [Temple of Sol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sol_Invictus#Aurelian).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBall2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid73-JAgAAQBAJpgPA81_81\]-322][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESartre2005\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidMNSyT_PuYVMCpgPA515_515\]-326] Palmyrene buildings were smashed, residents massacred and the Temple of Bel pillaged.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBall2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid73-JAgAAQBAJpgPA81_81\]-322]

Palmyra was significantly reduced and it largely disappeared from historical records of that period.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPollard2000\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid2ijMkjyG3cQCpgPA299_299\]-327] After its sacking, Aurelian repaired the Temple of Bel, and the [Legio I Illyricorum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Roman_legions#Late_Empire_legions) was stationed in the city.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPollard2000\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid2ijMkjyG3cQCpgPA298_298\]-166] Shortly before 303 the Camp of Diocletian, a *[castrum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Castrum)* in the western part of the city, was built.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPollard2000\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid2ijMkjyG3cQCpgPA298_298\]-166] The 4-hectare (9.9-acre) camp was a base for the Legio I Illyricorum,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPollard2000\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid2ijMkjyG3cQCpgPA298_298\]-166] which guarded the trade routes around the city.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPollard2000\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid2ijMkjyG3cQCpgPA299_299\]-327] Though some of the city would not be rebuilt, Palmyra would become a major stronghold and fortress in the East. Thanks in part to this, in the following years Palmyra began to regain importance, becoming a Christian city in the decades following its destruction by Aurelian.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStoneman1994\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid8kLFfE1qPhICpgPA190_190\]-328] In late 527, [Justinian I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justinian_I) further strengthened the city, ordering the restoration of Palmyra's churches and public buildings to protect the empire against raids by [Lakhmid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lakhmids) king [Al-Mundhir III ibn al-Nu'man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Mundhir_III_ibn_al-Nu'man).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGreatrexLieu2005\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidawCGAgAAQBAJpgPA85_85\]-329]

### Arab caliphates

Palmyra was conquered by the [Rashidun Caliphate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashidun_Caliphate) after its 634 capture by the [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim) general [Khalid ibn al-Walid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khalid_ibn_al-Walid), who took the city on his way to [Damascus](8914.md); an 18-day march by his [army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashidun_army) through the [Syrian Desert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Desert) from [Mesopotamia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesopotamia).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBurns2007\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidpoCBAgAAQBAJpgPA99_99\]-330] By then Palmyra was limited to the Diocletian camp.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESpeake1996\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidR44VRnNCzAYCpgPA568_568\]-95] After the conquest, the city became part of [Homs Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jund_Hims).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELe_Strange189036-331]

#### Umayyad and early Abbasid periods

Palmyra prospered as part of the Umayyad Caliphate, and its population grew.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHillenbrand1999\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidQ6ppv17CP6ECpgPA87_87\]-332] It was a key stop on the East-West trade route, with a large *[souq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Souq)* (market), built by the Umayyads,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHillenbrand1999\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidQ6ppv17CP6ECpgPA87_87\]-332][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBacharach1996\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidNdCTI5FqayACpgPA31_31\]-333] who also commissioned part of the Temple of Bel as a jama masjid ([congregational mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congregational_mosque)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBacharach1996\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidNdCTI5FqayACpgPA31_31\]-333] During this period, Palmyra was a stronghold of the [Banu Kalb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banu_Kalb) tribe,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrabarHolodKnustadTrousdale1978\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidQMSCNQlywSsCpgPA156_156\]-57] which began to take abode in and around the city after the conquest.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECaskel1966369-334] After being defeated by [Marwan II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marwan_II) during a [civil war in the caliphate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_Muslim_Civil_War), Umayyad contender [Sulayman ibn Hisham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulayman_ibn_Hisham) fled to the Banu Kalb in Palmyra, but eventually pledged allegiance to Marwan in 744; Palmyra continued to oppose Marwan until the surrender of the Banu Kalb chief [al-Asbagh ibn Dhu'ala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Asbagh_ibn_Dhu'ala) in 745.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIntagliata2018140-335] That year, Marwan ordered the city's walls demolished.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESpeake1996\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidR44VRnNCzAYCpgPA568_568\]-95][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECobb2001\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid2C6KIBw4F9YCpgPA73_73\]-336]

In 750 a revolt, led by [Majza'a ibn al-Kawthar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_al-Ward) and Umayyad pretender [Abu Muhammad al-Sufyani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Muhammad_al-Sufyani), against the new [Abbasid Caliphate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abbasid_Caliphate) swept across Syria;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECobb2001\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid2C6KIBw4F9YCpgPA47_47\]-337] the tribes in Palmyra supported the rebels.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECobb2001\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid2C6KIBw4F9YCpgPA48_48\]-338] After his defeat Abu Muhammad took refuge in the city, which withstood an Abbasid assault long enough to allow him to escape.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECobb2001\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid2C6KIBw4F9YCpgPA48_48\]-338]

#### Decentralization

Stone wall, with an arch and pillars

Fortifications at the Temple of Bel

Abbasid power dwindled during the 10th century, when the empire disintegrated and was divided among a number of vassals.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolt2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidTqasAgAAQBAJpgPA13_13\]-339] Most of the new rulers acknowledged the caliph as their nominal sovereign, a situation which continued until the [Mongol destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Baghdad_\(1258\)) of the Abbasid Caliphate in 1258.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELoewe1923\[httpsarchiveorgstreamcambridgemedieva04buryuoftpage300mode2up_300\]-340]

The population of the city began to decline in the ninth century, a process that continued throughout the tenth century, by the end of which the inhabitants occupied only the enclosed area of the Temple of Bel.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMajcherek2012478-341][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMeyer2017a72-342] In 955 [Sayf al-Dawla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sayf_al-Dawla), the [Hamdanid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamdanid_dynasty) prince of [Aleppo](159244.md), defeated the nomads near the city,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrabarHolodKnustadTrousdale1978\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidQMSCNQlywSsCpgPA11_11\]-343] and built a [kasbah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kasbah) (fortress) in response to [campaigns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab%E2%80%93Byzantine_wars#Byzantine_resurgence,_863%E2%80%9311th_century) by the Byzantine emperors [Nikephoros II Phokas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikephoros_II_Phokas) and [John I Tzimiskes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_I_Tzimiskes).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrabarHolodKnustadTrousdale1978\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidQMSCNQlywSsCpgPA158_158\]-344] With the advent of [Fatimid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fatimid_Caliphate) rule in the late 10th century, Palmyra remained a stronghold of the Kalb and taxes on the oasis' crops was a major source of the tribe's income. Toward the end of the century, the Kalb around Palmyra migrated from the area.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBianquis1989440,_641-345] Earthquakes devastated Palmyra in 1068 and 1089.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESpeake1996\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidR44VRnNCzAYCpgPA568_568\]-95][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFowden1999\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidUC5v4mgERxwCpgPA184_184\]-346] In the 1070s Syria was conquered by the [Seljuk Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seljuk_Empire),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChamberlain2005\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid1ioTXW3316ACpgPA148_148\]-347] and in 1082, the district of Homs came under the control of the Arab lord [Khalaf ibn Mula'ib](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khalaf_ibn_Mula'ib).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEÉlisséeff2007\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidUB4uSVt3ulUCpgPA158_158\]-348] The latter was a brigand and was removed and imprisoned in 1090 by the Seljuq sultan [Malik-Shah I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malik-Shah_I).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEÉlisséeff2007\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidUB4uSVt3ulUCpgPA158_158\]-348][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIbn_al-ʻAdīm1988\[httpshamelawsbrowsephpbook-10798page-3283_3354\]-349] Khalaf's lands were given to Malik-Shah's brother, [Tutush I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tutush_I),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIbn_al-ʻAdīm1988\[httpshamelawsbrowsephpbook-10798page-3283_3354\]-349] who gained his independence after his brother's 1092 death and established a [cadet branch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Seljuk_rulers_in_Syria_\(1076%E2%80%931117\)) of the Seljuk dynasty in Syria.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHanne2007\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidnDVOR7jZYJECpgPA135_135\]-350]

Ruins of an old stone castle

Fakhr-al-Din al-Maani Castle

In the twelfth century, Palmyra was ruled by [Toghtekin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toghtekin), the [Burid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burid_dynasty) [atabeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atabeg) of Damascus, who appointed his nephew governor.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGibb2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid5ds1vuFg8j0CpgPA178_178\]-351] Toghtekin's nephew was killed by rebels, and the atabeg retook the city in 1126.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGibb2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid5ds1vuFg8j0CpgPA178_178\]-351] Palmyra was given to Toghtekin's grandson, [Shihab-ud-din Mahmud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shihab-ud-din_Mahmud?action=edit\&redlink=1),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGibb2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid5ds1vuFg8j0CpgPA178_178\]-351] who was replaced by governor [Yusuf ibn Firuz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yusuf_ibn_Firuz) when Shihab-ud-din Mahmud returned to Damascus after his father [Taj al-Muluk Buri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taj_al-Muluk_Buri) succeeded Toghtekin.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIbn_al-Qalanisi1983\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidO94rCwAAQBAJpgPT378_386\]-352] The Burids transformed the Temple of Bel into a citadel in 1132, fortifying the city,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrabarHolodKnustadTrousdale1978\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidQMSCNQlywSsCpgPA161_161\]-353][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGibb2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid5ds1vuFg8j0CpgPA237_237\]-354] and transferring it to the [Bin Qaraja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bin_Qaraja?action=edit\&redlink=1) family three years later in exchange for Homs.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGibb2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid5ds1vuFg8j0CpgPA237_237\]-354]

During the mid-twelfth century, Palmyra was ruled by the [Zengid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zengid_dynasty) king [Nur ad-Din Mahmud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nur_ad-Din,_atabeg_of_Aleppo).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIbn_'Asakir1995\[httpshamelawsbrowsephpbook-71page-26366_121\]-355] It became part of the district of Homs,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEByliński1999161-356] which was given as a fiefdom to the Ayyubid general [Shirkuh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shirkuh) in 1168 and confiscated after his death in 1169.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEhrenkreutz1972\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidG9jnL131V3UCpgPA46_46\],_\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidG9jnL131V3UCpgPA72_72\]-357] Homs region was conquered by the [Ayyubid sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayyubid_dynasty) in 1174;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHamilton2005\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidIySQoHdviNkCpgPA98_98\]-358] the following year, [Saladin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saladin) gave Homs (including Palmyra) to his cousin [Nasir al-Din Muhammad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_ibn_Shirkuh) as a fiefdom.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHumphreys1977\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidJfXl5kvabhoCpgPA51_51\]-359] After Saladin's death, the Ayyubid realm was divided and Palmyra was given to Nasir al-Din Muhammad's son [Al-Mujahid Shirkuh II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Mujahid) (who built the castle of Palmyra known as [Fakhr-al-Din al-Maani Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fakhr-al-Din_al-Maani_Castle) around 1230).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMajor2001\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid1m4fbJyQ4pkCpgPA62_62\]-360][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBurns2009\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidz_IBAwAAQBAJpgPA243_243\]-361] Five years earlier, Syrian geographer [Yaqut al-Hamawi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yaqut_al-Hamawi) described Palmyra's residents as living in "a castle surrounded by a stone wall".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELe_Strange1890541-362]

### Mamluk period

Palmyra was used as a refuge by Shirkuh II's grandson, [al-Ashraf Musa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ashraf_Musa,_Emir_of_Homs), who allied himself with the [Mongol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongol_Empire) king [Hulagu Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hulagu_Khan) and fled after the Mongol defeat in the 1260 [Battle of Ain Jalut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Ain_Jalut) against the [Mamluks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mamluk_Sultanate_\(Cairo\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHumphreys1977\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidJfXl5kvabhoCpgPA360_360\]-363] Al-Ashraf Musa asked the Mamluk sultan [Qutuz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qutuz) for pardon and was accepted as a vassal.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHumphreys1977\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidJfXl5kvabhoCpgPA360_360\]-363] Al-Ashraf Musa died in 1263 without an heir, bringing the Homs district under direct Mamluk rule.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolt1995\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidb2oeolaGUCECpgPA38_38\]-364]

#### Al Fadl principality

Date trees, with Palmyra in the background

Palmyra's gardens

The [Al Fadl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Fadl) clan (a branch of the [Tayy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tayy) tribe) were loyal to the Mamluks, and in 1281, Prince [Issa bin Muhanna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isa_ibn_Muhanna) of the Al Fadl was appointed lord of Palmyra by sultan [Qalawun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Mansur_Qalawun).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEQīṭāz2007\[httpswebarchiveorgweb20221104070406httpwwwarab-encycomsyencydetails824719D985D987D986D8A7D8A3D8B3D8B1D8A9_788\]-365] Issa was succeeded in 1284 by his son [Muhanna bin Issa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhanna_ibn_Isa) who was imprisoned by sultan [al-Ashraf Khalil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ashraf_Khalil) in 1293, and restored two years later by sultan [al-Adil Kitbugha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Adil_Kitbugha).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEal-Ziriklī2002\[httpshamelawsbrowsephpbook-12286page-6880_316\]-366] Muhanna declared his loyalty to [Öljaitü](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%96ljait%C3%BC) of the [Ilkhanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilkhanate) in 1312 and was dismissed and replaced with his brother [Fadl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fadl_ibn_Isa) by sultan [an-Nasir Muhammad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/An-Nasir_Muhammad).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEal-Ziriklī2002\[httpshamelawsbrowsephpbook-12286page-6880_316\]-366] Although Muhanna was forgiven by an-Nasir and restored in 1317, he and his tribe were expelled in 1320 for his continued relations with the Ilkhanate,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEal-Ziriklī2002\[httpshamelawsbrowsephpbook-12286page-6881_317\]-367] and he was replaced by tribal chief [Muhammad ibn Abi Bakr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_ibn_Abi_Bakr_\(chief\)?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIbn_Khaldūn1988\[httpshamelawsbrowsephpbook-12320page-3301_501\]-368]

Muhanna was forgiven and restored by an-Nasir in 1330; he remained loyal to the sultan until his death in 1335, when he was succeeded by his son.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIbn_Khaldūn1988\[httpshamelawsbrowsephpbook-12320page-3301_501\]-368] Contemporary historian [Ibn Fadlallah al-Omari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shihab_al-Umari) described the city as having "vast gardens, flourishing trades and bizarre monuments".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEal-ʻUmarī2002\[httpshamelawsbrowsephpbook-11790page-458_528\]-369] The Al Fadl clan protected the trade routes and villages from [Bedouin](61770.md) raids,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIbn_Battuta1997\[httpshamelawsbrowsephpbook-11769page-383_413\]-370] raiding other cities and fighting among themselves.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIbn_Khaldūn1988\[httpshamelawsbrowsephpbook-12320page-3302_502\]-371] The Mamluks intervened militarily several times, dismissing, imprisoning or expelling its leaders.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIbn_Khaldūn1988\[httpshamelawsbrowsephpbook-12320page-3302_502\]-371] In 1400 Palmyra was attacked by [Timur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timur); the Fadl prince [Nu'air](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_bin_Hayyar?action=edit\&redlink=1) escaped the battle and later fought [Jakam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_rulers_of_Aleppo#Mamluks), the sultan of Aleppo.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEal-ʻAsqalānī1969\[httpshamelawsbrowsephpbook-26317page-885_350\]-372] Nu'air was captured, taken to Aleppo and executed in 1406; this, according to [Ibn Hajar al-Asqalani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibn_Hajar_al-Asqalani), ended the Al Fadl clan's power.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEal-ʻAsqalānī1969\[httpshamelawsbrowsephpbook-26317page-885_350\]-372][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEQīṭāz2007\[httpswebarchiveorgweb20221104070406httpwwwarab-encycomsyencydetails824719D985D987D986D8A7D8A3D8B3D8B1D8A9_788\]-365]

### Ottoman era

Painting of Palmyra

[Gerard Hofsted van Essen](https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerard%20Hofsted%20van%20Essen)'s painting of the ruins, following his participation in the 1691 [Levant Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levant_Company) expedition, the first Western expedition to the site. This is the earliest Western depiction of Palmyra. A group stands in the center foreground around a large stone, on which the date 1693 is shown. The gold text at the top explains the 1743 donation of the picture as part of the [Papenbroek Collection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papenbroek_Collection). Scroll left and right to view the full panorama.

While most of Syria came under [Ottoman](22278.md) rule in 1516, Palmyra (Tadmur) does not appear to have been incorporated into the Empire before the conquest of [Iraq](7515928.md) in 1534–1535. It first appears as the centre of an administrative district ([sanjak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanjak)) around 1560.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWinter201989–90-373][^cite_note-375][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEÇelebi1834\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid66hCAAAAcAAJpgPA93_93\]-374] The region was important to the Ottomans above all for its salt deposits. In 1568, the governor of the sancak restored the medieval citadel.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWinter201990–91-376] After 1568 the Ottomans appointed the [Lebanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_people) emir [Ali bin Musa Harfush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harfush_clan) as governor of Palmyra's sanjak,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWinter2010\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidKGeuAeFFJCECpgPA43_43\]-377] dismissing him in 1584 for insubordination.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWinter2010\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidKGeuAeFFJCECpgPA48_48\]-378] In 1630 Palmyra came under the tax authority of another Lebanese emir, [Fakhr-al-Din II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fakhr-al-Din_II),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarris2012\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXwUTDAAAQBAJpgPA103_103\]-379] who renovated Shirkuh II's castle (which became known as Fakhr-al-Din al-Maani Castle).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBurns2009\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidz_IBAwAAQBAJpgPA243_243\]-361][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEByliński1995146-380] The prince fell from grace with the Ottomans in 1633 and lost control of the village,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarris2012\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXwUTDAAAQBAJpgPA103_103\]-379] which remained a separate sanjak until it was absorbed by [Zor Sanjak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zor_Sanjak) in 1857.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPeters1911\[\[:s:1911_Encyclopædia_Britannica/Deir|933\]\]-381] The Ottoman governor of [Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syria_Vilayet), [Mehmed Rashid Pasha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehmed_Rashid_Pasha), established a garrison in the village to control the Bedouin in 1867.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHudson200825-382][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKennedyRiley2004\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidg1eQAgAAQBAJpgPA143_143\]-383]

### 20th century

People in an alley, with ruins in the background

The village, within the Temple of Bel, during the early 20th century

In 1918, as [World War I](4764461.md) was ending, the [Royal Air Force](25679.md) built an airfield for two planes,[^cite_note-385][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrainger2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidLG2cG5SshpECpgPA228_228\]-384] and in November the Ottomans retreated from Zor Sanjak without a fight.[^cite_note-387][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEQaddūrī2000\[httplibrarytebyannetarViewerText113382116_38\]-386] The [Syrian Emirate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Kingdom_of_Syria#The_Arab_constitutional_government)'s army entered [Deir ez-Zor](1429146.md) on 4 December, and Zor Sanjak became part of Syria.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEQaddūrī2000\[httplibrarytebyannetarViewerText113382118_40\]-388] In 1919, as the British and French argued over the borders of the planned [mandates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/League_of_Nations_mandate),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrainger2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidLG2cG5SshpECpgPA228_228\]-384] the British permanent military representative to the [Supreme War Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_War_Council) [Henry Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sir_Henry_Wilson,_1st_Baronet) suggested adding Palmyra to the [British mandate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Mandate_for_Mesopotamia_\(legal_instrument\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrainger2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidLG2cG5SshpECpgPA228_228\]-384] However, the British general [Edmund Allenby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmund_Allenby,_1st_Viscount_Allenby) persuaded his government to abandon this plan.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrainger2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidLG2cG5SshpECpgPA228_228\]-384] Syria (including Palmyra) became part of the [French Mandate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Mandate_for_Syria_and_the_Lebanon) after Syria's defeat in the [Battle of Maysalun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Maysalun) on 24 July 1920.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENeep2012\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid1Z7TbtGaAX8CpgPA28_28\]-389]

With Palmyra gaining importance in the French efforts to pacify the [Syrian Desert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Desert), a base was constructed in the village near the Temple of Bel in 1921.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENeep2012\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid1Z7TbtGaAX8CpgPA142_142\]-390] In 1929, Henri Seyrig, began excavating the ruins and convinced the villagers to move to a new, French-built village next to the site.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDarke2010\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidjv2jHT_GRe0CpgPA257_257\]-391] The relocation was completed in 1932;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStoneman1994\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid8kLFfE1qPhICpgPA12_12\]-392] ancient Palmyra was ready for excavation as its villagers settled into the new village of [Tadmur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tadmur).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDrijvers1976\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidmuQ3AAAAIAAJpgPA4_4\]-393][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDarke2010\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidjv2jHT_GRe0CpgPA257_257\]-391] During [World War II](32927.md), the Mandate came under the authority of [Vichy France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vichy_France),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMoubayed2012\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid2UANAgAAQBAJpgPA46_46\]-394] who gave permission to [Nazi Germany](21212.md) to use the airfield at Palmyra;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatson2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksido4vrUbMK5eECpgPA80_80\]-395] forces of [Free France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_France), backed by British forces, invaded Syria in June 1941,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMoubayed2012\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid2UANAgAAQBAJpgPA46_46\]-394] and on 3 July 1941, the British took control over the city in the aftermath of a [battle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Palmyra_\(1941\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECave2012\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid-3y-BAAAQBAJpgPA55_55\]-396]

### Syrian civil war

Further information: [Palmyra offensive (May 2015)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palmyra_offensive_\(May_2015\)), [Palmyra offensive (March 2016)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palmyra_offensive_\(March_2016\)), [Palmyra offensive (December 2016)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palmyra_offensive_\(December_2016\)), and [Palmyra offensive (2017)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palmyra_offensive_\(2017\))

Destructions in Palmyra





Sculpture in the Palmyra Museum, before and after the conflict.

During the war the [Syrian Army](3423927.md) positioned its troops within the archaeological site.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidt-Colinet202021-168] On 13 May 2015, IS launched an attack on the modern town of Tadmur, sparking fears that the [iconoclastic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iconoclasm) group would destroy the adjacent ancient site of Palmyra.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMackay2015-397] The Syrian Directorate-General of Antiquities and Museums removed more than 400 statues and hundreds of artifacts from the museum and several safe locations, transporting them to Damascus for safekeeping.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAbdulkarim2020115-154] A number of Greco-Roman busts, jewelry, and other objects looted from the museum have been later found on the international market.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcGirk2015-398] IS' forces entered Palmyra on 21 May in a sudden takeover,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShaheen2015-399] which abruptly ended the Directorate-General's efforts to evacuate the remaining artifacts consisting of large statues and pieces.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAbdulkarim2020115-154]

During IS' first occupation of the site, Palmyra's theatre was used as a place of public executions of their opponents and captives; videos were released by ISIL showing the killing of Syrian prisoners in front of crowds at the theatre.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESaul2015-400][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECarissimo2015-401] On 18 August, Palmyra's retired antiquities chief [Khaled al-Asaad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khaled_al-Asaad) was beheaded by ISIL after being tortured for a month to extract information about the city and its treasures; al-Asaad refused to give any information to his captors.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWithnall2015-402]

The Syrian Army supported by [Russian airstrikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_military_intervention_in_the_Syrian_Civil_War) recaptured Palmyra on 27 March 2016.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPlets201718-403] According to initial reports, the damage to the archaeological site was less extensive than anticipated, with numerous structures still standing.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGambino2016-404] Russian de-mining teams began clearing mines planted by IS prior to their retreat.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMakieh2016-405] Following heavy fighting, IS briefly reoccupied the city on 11 December 2016,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2016-406] prompting an offensive by the Syrian Army which retook the city on 2 March 2017.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDearden2017-407] On 7 December 2024, Palmyra was [captured](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palmyra_offensive_\(2024\)) by the [Syrian Free Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Free_Army), a day before the [fall of the Assad regime](78548066.md). The [Syrian transitional government](79584988.md) stationed guards as to protect the area from further damage.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEArraf2025-408]

## Government

Inscription on a stone pillar

Inscription in Greek and Aramaic honoring the *[strategos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strategos)* Zabdilas, whose Roman name was Julius Aurelius Zenobius, dated 242–243 AD.

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMillar2006\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDFLqCQAAQBAJpgPA205_205\]-409]

From the beginning of its history to the first century AD Palmyra was a petty sheikhdom,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBall200956-410] and by the first century BC a Palmyrene identity began to develop.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA125_125\]-411] During the first half of the first century AD, Palmyra incorporated some of the institutions of a Greek city (polis);[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA122_122\]-234] the notion of an existing citizenship first appears in an inscription, dated to AD 10, mentioning the "people of Palmyra".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA126_126\]-412] In AD 74, an inscription mentions the city's boule (senate).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA122_122\]-234] The tribal role in Palmyra is debated; during the first century, four treasurers representing the four tribes seems to have partially controlled the administration but their role became ceremonial by the second century and power rested in the hands of the council.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEdwell2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDQgmOZlsEWcCpgPA48_48\]-413]

The Palmyrene council consisted of about six hundred members of the local elite (such as the elders or heads of wealthy families or clans),[^cite_note-414][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA127_127\]-233] representing the city's four-quarters.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDirven1999\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid_LfXg2r6FT0CpgPA74_74\]-86] The council, headed by a president,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA128_128\]-415] managed civic responsibilities;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA127_127\]-233] it supervised public works (including the construction of public buildings), approved expenditures, collected taxes,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA127_127\]-233] and appointed two [archons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eponymous_archon) (lords) each year.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA128_128\]-415][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidwnTOBAAAQBAJpgPA43_43\]-416] Palmyra's military was led by *[strategoi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strategos)* (generals) appointed by the council.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA129_129\]-417][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYoung2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidE5yCAgAAQBAJpgPA145_145\]-418] Roman provincial authority set and approved Palmyra's tariff structure,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEdwell2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDQgmOZlsEWcCpgPA54_54\]-419] but the provincial interference in local government was kept minimal as the empire sought to ensure the continuous success of Palmyrene trade most beneficial to Rome.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEdwell2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDQgmOZlsEWcCpgPA49_49\]-420] An imposition of direct provincial administration would have jeopardized Palmyra's ability to conduct its trading activities in the East, especially in Parthia.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEdwell2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDQgmOZlsEWcCpgPA49_49\]-420]

With the elevation of Palmyra to a [colonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonia_\(Roman\)) around 213–216, the city ceased being subject to Roman provincial governors and taxes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEClineGraham2011\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid1eRb5h7ATbsCpgPA271_271\]-421] Palmyra incorporated Roman institutions into its system while keeping many of its former ones.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA130_130\]-422] The council remained, and the *strategos* designated one of two annually-elected magistrates.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA130_130\]-422] This *[duumviri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duumviri)* implemented the new colonial constitution,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA130_130\]-422] replacing the archons.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidwnTOBAAAQBAJpgPA43_43\]-416] Palmyra's political scene changed with the rise of Odaenathus and his family; an inscription dated to 251 describes Odaenathus' son Hairan I as "Ras" (lord) of Palmyra (exarch in the Greek section of the inscription) and another inscription dated to 252 describes Odaenathus with the same title.[^cite_note-423][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidecfiAAAAQBAJpgPA44_44\]-268] Odaenathus was probably elected by the council as exarch,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBall2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid73-JAgAAQBAJpgPA77_77\]-269] which was an unusual title in the Roman empire and was not part of the traditional Palmyrene governance institutions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidecfiAAAAQBAJpgPA44_44\]-268][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMackay2004\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid6rLDy6qqi0UCpgPA272_272\]-424] Whether Odaenathus' title indicated a military or a priestly position is unknown,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA131_131\]-425] but the military role is more likely.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMennen2011\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksiduZqQjkb07g8CpgPA224_224\]-426] By 257 Odaenathus was known as a *[consularis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consularis)*, possibly the *[legatus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legatus)* of the province of Phoenice.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA131_131\]-425] In 258 Odaenathus began extending his political influence, taking advantage of regional instability caused by Sasanian aggression;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA131_131\]-425] this culminated in the Battle of Edessa,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA177_177\]-270] Odaenathus' royal elevation and mobilization of troops, which made Palmyra a kingdom.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA177_177\]-270]

The monarchy continued most civic institutions,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA131_131\]-425][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESivertsev2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidOfWUkVoHP7YCpgPA72_72\]-427] but the duumviri and the council were no longer attested after 264; Odaenathus appointed a governor for the city.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHartmann201664-428] In the absence of the monarch, the city was administered by a [viceroy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viceroy).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECooke1903\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidKwsIAwAAQBAJpgPA286_286\]-429] Although governors of the eastern Roman provinces under Odaenathus' control were still appointed by Rome, the king had overall authority.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidwnTOBAAAQBAJpgPA75_75\]-430] During Zenobia's rebellion, governors were appointed by the queen.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidecfiAAAAQBAJpgPA115_115\]-431] Not all Palmyrenes accepted the dominion of the royal family; a senator, Septimius Haddudan, appears in a later Palmyrene inscription as aiding Aurelian's armies during the 273 rebellion.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEButcher2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidYJPn3-rRjC0CpgPA60_60\]-432][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatson2004\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidkJ2JAgAAQBAJpgPA81_81\]-433] After the Roman destruction of the city, Palmyra was ruled directly by Rome,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShahîd1984\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidW4H97SA6pMACpgPA15_15\]-434] and then by a succession of other rulers, including the Burids and Ayyubids,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGibb2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid5ds1vuFg8j0CpgPA178_178\]-351][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHumphreys1977\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidJfXl5kvabhoCpgPA51_51\]-359] and subordinate Bedouin chiefs—primarily the Fadl family, who governed for the Mamluks.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIrwin2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidaxNbLoiLLgMCpgPA256_256\]-435]

### Military

Stone relief depicting warriors

Relief in the Temple of Bel depicting Palmyrene war gods



Palmyrene horseman, in a hunting scene.

Due to its military character and efficiency in battle, Palmyra was described by [Irfan Shahîd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irfan_Shah%C3%AEd) as the "[Sparta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sparta) among the cities of the Orient, Arab and other, and even its gods were represented dressed in military uniforms."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShahîd1984\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidW4H97SA6pMACpgPA38_38\]-436] Palmyra's army protected the city and its economy, helping extend Palmyrene authority beyond the city walls and protecting the countryside's desert trade routes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA143_143\]-437] The city had a substantial military;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA284_284\]-229] Zabdibel commanded a force of 10,000 in the third century BC,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA278_278\]-47] and Zenobia led an army of 70,000 in the Battle of Emesa.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHartmann2001\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidBdcHK8Ll1jMCpgPA371_371\]-438] Soldiers were recruited from the city and its territories, spanning several thousand square kilometers from the outskirts of Homs to the Euphrates valley.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA284_284\]-229] Non-Palmyrene soldiers were also recruited; a [Nabatean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nabataeans) cavalryman is recorded in 132 as serving in a Palmyrene unit stationed at [Anah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anah).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYoung2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidE5yCAgAAQBAJpgPA124_124\]-20] Palmyra's recruiting system is unknown; the city might have selected and equipped the troops and the *strategoi* led, trained and disciplined them.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidecfiAAAAQBAJpgPA26_26\]-439]

The *strategoi* were appointed by the council with the approval of Rome.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYoung2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidE5yCAgAAQBAJpgPA145_145\]-418] The royal army in the mid 3rd century AD was under the leadership of the monarch aided by generals,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPotter2010\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidg4ZmqsyC5kECpgPA162_162\]-440][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStoneman1994\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid8kLFfE1qPhICpgPA122_122\]-441] and was modeled on the [Sasanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_of_the_Sasanian_Empire) in arms and tactics.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBall2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid73-JAgAAQBAJpgPA86_86\]-107] The Palmyrenes were noted archers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA289_289\]-442] They used infantry while a heavily armored cavalry ([clibanarii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clibanarii)) constituted the main attacking force.[^cite_note-444][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidecfiAAAAQBAJpgPA24_24\]-445][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDixonSouthern2005\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidik9S7vNkQzkCpgPA76_76\]-446] Palmyra's infantry was armed with swords, lances and small round shields;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidecfiAAAAQBAJpgPA25_25\]-245] the clibanarii were fully armored (including their horses), and used heavy spears ([kontos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kontos_\(weapon\))) 3.65 metres (12.0 ft) long without shields.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDixonSouthern2005\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidik9S7vNkQzkCpgPA76_76\]-446][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFields200818-447]

#### Relations with Rome

Citing the Palmyrenes' combat skills in large, sparsely populated areas, the Romans formed a Palmyrene [auxilia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auxilia) to serve in the [Imperial Roman army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperial_Roman_army).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidecfiAAAAQBAJpgPA25_25\]-245] Vespasian reportedly had 8,000 Palmyrene archers in Judea,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidecfiAAAAQBAJpgPA25_25\]-245] and Trajan established the first Palmyrene Auxilia in 116 (a [camel cavalry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camel_cavalry) unit, Ala I Ulpia dromedariorum Palmyrenorum).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidecfiAAAAQBAJpgPA25_25\]-245][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStoneman1994\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid8kLFfE1qPhICpgPA27_27\]-448][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWheeler2011\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid1D612o_X2VYCpgPA258_258\]-449] Palmyrene units were deployed throughout the Roman Empire,[^cite_note-451] serving in [Dacia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Dacia) late in Hadrian's reign,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEdwell2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDQgmOZlsEWcCpgPA52_52\]-452] and at [El Kantara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Kantara) in [Numidia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Numidia) and [Moesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moesia) under [Antoninus Pius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antoninus_Pius).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEdwell2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDQgmOZlsEWcCpgPA52_52\]-452][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDirven1999\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid_LfXg2r6FT0CpgPA181_181\]-453] During the late second century Rome formed the [Cohors XX Palmyrenorum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cohors_XX_Palmyrenorum), which was stationed in Dura-Europos.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEdwell2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDQgmOZlsEWcCpgPA139_139\]-454]

## Religion

Relief of three human-appearing Palmyrene gods

Baalshamin (center), Aglibol (left) and Malakbel (right)

Palmyra's gods were primarily part of the [northwestern Semitic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canaanite_religion) [pantheon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pantheon_\(religion\)), with the addition of gods from the [Mesopotamian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Mesopotamian_religion) and [Arab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabian_mythology) pantheons.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELevick2007\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidBjkfKsCttrkCpgPA15_15\]-455] The city's chief pre-Hellenistic deity was called Bol,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETeixidor1979\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidTccUAAAAIAAJpgPR1_1\]-456] an abbreviation of [Baal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baal) (a northwestern Semitic honorific).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDrijvers1980\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid69YUAAAAIAAJpgPA46_46\]-457] The Babylonian cult of [Bel-Marduk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bel-Marduk) influenced the Palmyrene religion and by 217 BC the chief deity's name was changed to [Bel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bel_\(mythology\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETeixidor1979\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidTccUAAAAIAAJpgPR1_1\]-456] This did not indicate the replacing of the northwestern Semitic Bol with a Mesopotamian deity, but was a mere change in the name.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDrijvers1980\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid69YUAAAAIAAJpgPA46_46\]-457]

Second in importance, after the supreme deity,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWaardenburg2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidrMmsS7tr1AECpgPA33_33\]-458] were over sixty ancestral gods of the Palmyrene clans.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWaardenburg2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidrMmsS7tr1AECpgPA33_33\]-458][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDirven199883-459] Palmyra had unique deities,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEButcher2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidYJPn3-rRjC0CpgPA345_345\]-460] such as the god of justice and Efqa's guardian [Yarhibol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yarhibol),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA64_64\]-461][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA66_66\]-462] the sun god [Malakbel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malakbel),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETeixidor1979\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidTccUAAAAIAAJpgPR52_52\]-463] and the moon god [Aglibol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aglibol).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETeixidor1979\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidTccUAAAAIAAJpgPR52_52\]-463] Palmyrenes worshiped regional deities, including the greater [Levantine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levant) gods [Astarte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astarte), [Baal-hamon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baal-hamon), Baalshamin and [Atargatis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atargatis);[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEButcher2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidYJPn3-rRjC0CpgPA345_345\]-460] the Babylonian gods Nabu and [Nergal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nergal),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEButcher2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidYJPn3-rRjC0CpgPA345_345\]-460] and the Arab [Azizos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azizos), [Arsu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arsu), [Šams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shamash) and Al-lāt.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEButcher2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidYJPn3-rRjC0CpgPA345_345\]-460][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA64_64\]-461]

The deities worshiped in the countryside were depicted as camel or horse riders and bore Arab names.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDrijvers1976\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidmuQ3AAAAIAAJpgPA4_4\]-393] The nature of those deities is uncertain as only names are known, most importantly [Abgal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abgal_\(god\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDrijvers1976\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidmuQ3AAAAIAAJpgPA21_21\]-464] The Palmyrene pantheon included [ginnaye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ginnaye?action=edit\&redlink=1) (some were given the designation "[Gad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gad_\(deity\))"),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEColledge1986\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidlSZfkzVk3GMCpgPA6_6\]-465] a group of lesser deities popular in the countryside,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWaardenburg1984\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidmQnv_Wh3gCwCpgPA273_273\]-466] who were similar to the Arab [jinn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jinn) and the Roman [genius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genius_\(mythology\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETeixidor1979\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidTccUAAAAIAAJpgPR77_77\]-467] Ginnaye were believed to have the appearance and behavior of humans, similar to Arab jinn.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETeixidor1979\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidTccUAAAAIAAJpgPR77_77\]-467] Unlike jinn, however, the ginnaye could not [possess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demonic_possession) or injure humans.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETeixidor1979\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidTccUAAAAIAAJpgPR77_77\]-467] Their role was similar to the Roman genius: [tutelary deities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tutelary_deity) who guarded individuals and their caravans, cattle and villages.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWaardenburg2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidrMmsS7tr1AECpgPA33_33\]-458][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETeixidor1979\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidTccUAAAAIAAJpgPR77_77\]-467]

Although the Palmyrenes worshiped their deities as individuals, some were associated with other gods.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDirven1999\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid_LfXg2r6FT0CpgPA159_159\]-468] Bel had [Astarte-Belti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astarte-Belti?action=edit\&redlink=1) as his consort, and formed a [triple deity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triple_deity) with Aglibol and Yarhibol (who became a sun god in his association with Bel).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA64_64\]-461][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDrijvers1976\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidmuQ3AAAAIAAJpgPA12_12\]-469] Malakbel was part of many associations,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDirven1999\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid_LfXg2r6FT0CpgPA159_159\]-468] pairing with [Gad Taimi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gad_Taimi?action=edit\&redlink=1) and Aglibol,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDirven1999\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid_LfXg2r6FT0CpgPA160_160\]-470][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDirven1999\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid_LfXg2r6FT0CpgPA160_160\]-470] and forming a triple deity with Baalshamin and Aglibol.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDirven1999\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid_LfXg2r6FT0CpgPA161_161\]-471] Palmyra hosted an [Akitu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akitu) (spring festival) each [Nisan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nisan).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDirven1999\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid_LfXg2r6FT0CpgPA146_146\],_\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid_LfXg2r6FT0CpgPA147_147\]-472] Each of the city's four-quarters had a sanctuary for a deity considered ancestral to the resident tribe; Malakbel and Aglibol's sanctuary was in the Komare quarter.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETeixidor1979\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidTccUAAAAIAAJpgPR36_36\]-473] The Baalshamin sanctuary was in the Ma'zin quarter, the Arsu sanctuary in the Mattabol quarter,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETeixidor1979\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidTccUAAAAIAAJpgPR36_36\]-473] and the Atargatis sanctuary in the fourth tribe's quarter.[^cite_note-474][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDirven1999\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid_LfXg2r6FT0CpgPA161_161\]-471]



An Altar found in [Trastevere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trastevere) dedicated to Malakbel bearing the epithet Sol Sanctissimus

The priests of Palmyra were selected from the city's leading families,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKaizer2005\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidkTxg2_vRM1YCpgPA179_179\]-475] and are recognized in busts through their headdresses which have the shape of a [polos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polos) adorned with laurel wreath or other tree made of bronze among other elements.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDrijvers1976\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidmuQ3AAAAIAAJpgPA22_22\]-476] The high priest of Bel's temple was the highest religious authority and headed the clergy of priests who were organized into collegia each headed by a higher priest.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright2004\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksiduvtebmqZZDYCpgPA296_296\]-477] The personnel of Efqa spring's sanctuary dedicated to Yarhibol belonged to a special class of priests as they were oracles.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright2004\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksiduvtebmqZZDYCpgPA296_296\]-477] Palmyra's paganism was replaced with Christianity as the religion spread across the Roman Empire, and a bishop was reported in the city by 325.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStoneman1994\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid8kLFfE1qPhICpgPA190_190\]-328] Although most temples became churches, the Temple of Al-lāt was destroyed in 385 at the order of [Maternus Cynegius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maternus_Cynegius) (the [eastern praetorian prefect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Praetorian_prefecture_of_the_East)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStoneman1994\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid8kLFfE1qPhICpgPA190_190\]-328] After the Muslim conquest in 634 [Islam](6037917.md) gradually replaced Christianity, and the last known bishop of Palmyra was consecrated after 818.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShahîd1995\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidBEvEV9OVzacCpgPA439_439\]-478]

### Malakbel and the Roman Sol Invictus

In 274, following his victory over Palmyra, Aurelian dedicated a large temple of [Sol Invictus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sol_Invictus) in Rome;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHijmans2009484-479] most scholars consider Aurelian's Sol Invictus to be of Syrian origin,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHijmans2009485-480] either a continuation of emperor [Elagabalus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elagabalus) cult of [Sol Invictus Elagabalus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elagabalus_\(deity\)), or Malakbel of Palmyra.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalsberghe1972\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid8tIUAAAAIAAJpgPA156_156\]-481] The Palmyrene deity was commonly identified with the Roman god [Sol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sol_\(Roman_mythology\)) and he had a temple dedicated for him on the right bank of the [Tiber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiber) since the second century.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatson2004\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidkJ2JAgAAQBAJpgPA196_196\]-482] Also, he bore the epithet Invictus and was known with the name Sol "Sanctissimus", the latter was an epithet Aurelian bore on an inscription from [Capena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capena).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatson2004\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidkJ2JAgAAQBAJpgPA196_196\]-482]

The position of the Palmyrene deity as Aurelian's Sol Invictus is inferred from a passage by [Zosimus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zosimus_\(historian\)) reading: "and the magnificent temple of the sun he (i.e. Aurelian) embellished with votive gifts from Palmyra, setting up statues of [Helios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helios) and Bel".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDirven1999\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid_LfXg2r6FT0CpgPA174_174\]-483] Three deities from Palmyra exemplified solar features: Malakbel, Yarhibol and Šams, hence the identification of the Palmyrene Helios appearing in Zosimus' work with Malakbel.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDirven1999\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid_LfXg2r6FT0CpgPA174_174\]-483] Some scholars criticize the notion of Malakbel's identification with Sol Invictus; according to [Gaston Halsberghe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaston_Halsberghe?action=edit\&redlink=1), the cult of Malakbel was too local for it to become an imperial Roman god and Aurelian's restoration of Bel's temple and sacrifices dedicated to Malakbel were a sign of his attachment to the sun god in general and his respect to the many ways in which the deity was worshiped.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalsberghe1972\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid8tIUAAAAIAAJpgPA157_157\]-484] [Richard Stoneman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Stoneman?action=edit\&redlink=1) suggested another approach in which Aurelian simply borrowed the imagery of Malakbel to enhance his own solar deity.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStoneman1994\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid8kLFfE1qPhICpgPA185_185\]-485] The relation between Malakbel and Sol Invictus can not be confirmed and will probably remain unresolved.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatson2004\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidkJ2JAgAAQBAJpgPA196_196\]-482]

## Economy

Ruins of two stone walls, with doorways

Palmyra's Agora; the two front entrances lead to the interior, the city's marketplace

Palmyra's economy before and at the beginning of the Roman period was based on agriculture, pastoralism, and commerce;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYoung2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidE5yCAgAAQBAJpgPA124_124\]-20] the city served as a rest station for the caravans which sporadically crossed the desert.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBall2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid73-JAgAAQBAJpgPA74_74\]-220] By the end of the first century BC, the city had a mixed economy based on agriculture, pastoralism, taxation,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA51_51\]-486][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStoneman1994\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid8kLFfE1qPhICpgPA57_57\]-487] and, most importantly, the caravan trade.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoward2012\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid6QPWXrCCzBICpgPA158_158\]-488] Taxation was an important source of revenue for the Palmyrene government.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStoneman1994\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid8kLFfE1qPhICpgPA57_57\]-487] Caravaneers paid taxes in the building known as the Tariff Court,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA282_282\]-82] where a tax law dating to AD 137 was exhibited.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGawlikowski2011420-146][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERostovtzeff.1932\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidr68SAAAAIAAJpgPA74_74\]-489] The law regulated the tariffs paid by the merchants for goods sold at the internal market or exported from the city.[^cite_note-491][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA282_282\]-82][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA70_70\]-492]

After Palmyra's destruction in 273, it became a market for villagers and nomads from the surrounding area.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStoneman1994\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid8kLFfE1qPhICpgPA189_189\]-493] The city regained some of its prosperity during the Umayyad era, indicated by the discovery of a large Umayyad *souq* in the colonnaded street.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKennedy2006\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXdFqgSBTYeYCpgPA54-IA30_296\]-494] Palmyra was a minor trading center until its destruction in 1400;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERobinson194610-495] according to [Sharaf ad-Din Ali Yazdi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharaf_ad-Din_Ali_Yazdi), Timur's men took 200,000 sheep,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIbn_Arabshah1986296-496] and the city was reduced into a settlement on the desert border whose inhabitants herded and cultivated small plots for vegetables and corn.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAddison1838\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid2Zy3NgMGcu0CpgPA333_333\]-497]

### Agriculture and irrigation in classical Palmyra

See also: [Canalizations of Zenobia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canalizations_of_Zenobia)

The classicist [Andrew M. Smith II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_M._Smith_II?action=edit\&redlink=1) suggested that most land in Palmyra was owned by the city, which collected grazing taxes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith_II2013\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidh5cMho6zFckCpgPA51_51\]-486] The oasis had about 1,000 hectares (2,500 acres) of irrigable land,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMétral2000\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidiwxeHaKUGFMCpgPA130_130\]-498] which surrounded the city.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidecfiAAAAQBAJpgPA27_27\]-499] The Palmyrenes constructed an extensive irrigation system in the northern mountains that consisted of reservoirs and channels to capture and store the occasional rainfall.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMeyer2013273-500] The most notable irrigation work is [Harbaqa Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harbaqa_Dam) which was constructed in the late first century AD;[^cite_note-501][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEButcher2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidYJPn3-rRjC0CpgPA163_163\]-502] it is located 48 km (30 mi) southwest of the city and can collect 140,000 cubic metres (4,900,000 cu ft) of water.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoffmann-Salz2015\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidHvApCgAAQBAJpgPA242_242\]-503] [Terebinth trees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pistacia_palaestina) in the hinterland were an important source of charcoal, resin and oil; although evidence is lacking, it is possible that olive trees were also planted, and dairy products were produced in the villages;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMeyer2013275-231] it is also apparent that barley was cultivated.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMeyer2013274-504] However, agriculture could not support the population and food was imported.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidecfiAAAAQBAJpgPA27_27\]-499]

### Commerce in classical Palmyra

See also: [Silk Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silk_Road)



Palmyra caravan. Palmyra Archaeological Museum

Map of the Silk Road, from China to Europe

The [Silk Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silk_Road) in Eurasia

If the Laghman II inscription in Afghanistan (commissioned by [Ashoka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashoka)) is referring to Palmyra, then the city's role in Central Asian overland trade was prominent as early as the third century BC.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKaizer2017\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidC7Q1DwAAQBAJpgPA34_34\]-212] During the first centuries AD, Palmyra's main trade route ran east to the Euphrates where it connected at the city of [Hīt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%C4%ABt).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcLaughlin2010\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidnTEdCgAAQBAJpgPA97_97\]-505] The route then ran south along the river toward the port of [Charax Spasinu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charax_Spasinu) on the [Persian Gulf](24761.md), where Palmyrene ships traveled back and forth to India.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYoung2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidE5yCAgAAQBAJpgPA133_133\]-506] Goods were imported from India, China and [Transoxiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transoxiana),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA283_283\]-507] and exported west to Emesa (or Antioch) then the Mediterranean ports,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStoneman1994\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid8kLFfE1qPhICpgPA19_19\]-508] from which they were distributed throughout the Roman Empire.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYoung2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidE5yCAgAAQBAJpgPA133_133\]-506] In addition to the usual route some Palmyrene merchants used the [Red Sea](26191.md#Ancient_era),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA283_283\]-507] probably as a result of the [Roman–Parthian Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman%E2%80%93Parthian_Wars).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDqMrR29Cc7MCpgPA111_111\]-509] Goods were carried overland from the seaports to a [Nile](21244.md) port, and then taken to the Egyptian Mediterranean ports for export.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDqMrR29Cc7MCpgPA111_111\]-509] Inscriptions attesting a Palmyrene presence in Egypt date to the reign of Hadrian.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHourani1995\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidZDh2KKSlQg4CpgPA34_34\]-510]

Since Palmyra was not on the main trading route (which followed the Euphrates),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYoung2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidE5yCAgAAQBAJpgPA124_124\]-20] the Palmyrenes secured the desert route passing their city.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYoung2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidE5yCAgAAQBAJpgPA124_124\]-20] They connected it to the Euphrates valley, providing water and shelter.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYoung2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidE5yCAgAAQBAJpgPA124_124\]-20] The Palmyrene route connected the [Silk Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silk_Road) with the Mediterranean,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoffmann-Salz2015\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidjfApCgAAQBAJpgPA234_234\]-511] and was used almost exclusively by the city's merchants,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYoung2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidE5yCAgAAQBAJpgPA124_124\]-20] who maintained a presence in many cities, including Dura-Europos in 33 BC,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEdwell2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDQgmOZlsEWcCpgPA36_36\]-247] [Babylon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babylon) by AD 19, [Seleucia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seleucia) by AD 24,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDirven1999\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid_LfXg2r6FT0CpgPA20_20\]-239] [Dendera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dendera), [Coptos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qift),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYoung2003\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidE5yCAgAAQBAJpgPA137_137\]-512] Bahrain, the [Indus River Delta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indus_River_Delta), [Merv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merv) and Rome.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBall2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid73-JAgAAQBAJpgPA76_76\]-513]

The caravan trade depended on patrons and merchants.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoward2012\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid6QPWXrCCzBICpgPA159_159\]-514] Patrons owned the land on which the caravan animals were raised, providing animals and guards for the merchants.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoward2012\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid6QPWXrCCzBICpgPA159_159\]-514] The lands were located in the numerous villages of the Palmyrene countryside.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDrijvers1976\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidmuQ3AAAAIAAJpgPA4_4\]-393] Although merchants used the patrons to conduct business, their roles often overlapped and a patron would sometimes lead a caravan.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoward2012\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid6QPWXrCCzBICpgPA159_159\]-514] Commerce made Palmyra and its merchants among the wealthiest in the region.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoward2012\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid6QPWXrCCzBICpgPA158_158\]-488] Some caravans were financed by a single merchant,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA282_282\]-82] such as Male' Agrippa (who financed Hadrian's visit in 129 and the 139 rebuilding of the Temple of Bel).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBryce2014\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidXno9AgAAQBAJpgPA279_279\]-253] The primary income-generating trade good was silk, which was exported from the East to the West.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStoneman1994\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid8kLFfE1qPhICpgPA59_59\]-515] Other exported goods included jade, muslin, spices, ebony, ivory and precious stones.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBall2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid73-JAgAAQBAJpgPA76_76\]-513] For its domestic market Palmyra imported a variety of goods including slaves, prostitutes, olive oil, dyed goods, myrrh and perfume.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStoneman1994\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid8kLFfE1qPhICpgPA58_58\]-490][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBall2002\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid73-JAgAAQBAJpgPA76_76\]-513]

## Research and excavations

A road of colonnades

The Colonnade

Four groups of four columns each

The Tetrapylon (destroyed in 2017)
